Bucyrus City Public Hearing

Tuesday, April 14, 2026

5:30 p.m. – 5:55 p.m.

Audio can be found at: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Wg5-4CRHkRM

President Kevin Myers, Jim Mee, Robert Taylor, Ray Thitoff, Christopher Mauritz,

Allison LeClair, Terry Spiegel, Cody Scott

Call to Order

President Kevin Myers called the Public Hearing to order at 5:30 p.m.

Roll Call:

Roll call was taken with the following members present: James Mee, Robert Taylor, Ray

Thitoff, Chris Mauritz, Allison LeClair and Cody Scott. Terry Spiegel was absent.

Introduction

President Kevin Myers introduced James Mee as Platting Committee Chair

Public Hearing Notification – President Kevin Myers informed Council and the Public that

the notice for the Public Hearing was published in the Telegraph Forum on March 12, 2026

(An incorrect date was listed on the agenda, but documentation was in the packet showing

it was in publication on March 12, 2026).

Items Under Consideration:

President Kevin Myers read the items of discussion as follows:

1) The Proposed zoning reclassification of 211 Hopley Avenue (Parcel numbers 8-

0015118.000, 8-0015119.00) from designation Urban Residential “R3”, to

Downtown Business District, “DB”

2) The Proposed zoning classification of BCO 1175 entitled Downtown Business,

1175.03, to allow for fueling stations and/or convenience stores to be considered

Conditional uses

3) The Proposed zoning reclassification of 1428 South Sandusky (Parcel numbers 8-

0016636.000, 8-0016637.000, and 8-0016638.000) from designation Limited

Industrial/Commercial, “LIC”, to Suburban Residential “R1”

4) The Proposed zoning reclassification of 1518 South Sandusky (Parcel numbers 8-

0015746.000, 8-0017244.000, and 8-0019017.000 from designation Limited

Industrial/Commercial, “LIC”, to Suburban Residential “R1”

5) The Proposed zoning reclassification of 1482 South Sandusky (Parcel numbers 8-

0019015.000, 8-0019016.000, and 8-0019017.000) from designation Limited

Industrial/Commercial, “LIC”, to Suburban Residential “R1”

Discussion:

Platting Committee Comments – None

Zoning Reclassification of 211 Hopley Avenue

Proponents – None

Opponents – Harold Howell, 3786 Knaus Road. Mr. Howell has concerns of the zoning

change. He states he will attend the neighborhood meeting on April 16, 2026 with his list of

concerns for discussion.

• Robert Taylor made a motion to refer the Zoning Reclassification of 211 Hopley

Avenue to the Platting Committee for further study.

• Cody Scott seconded the motion.

• Voice vote passes. Referred back to Platting Committee.

Zoning classification to allow for fueling stations and/or convenience stores

Proponents – None

Opponents – Harold Howell, 3786 Knaus Road. Mr. Howell has concerns of the zoning

change. He states he will attend the neighborhood meeting on April 16, 2026 with his list of

concerns for discussion.

• Robert Taylor made a motion to refer the Zoning classification to allow for fueling

stations and/or convenience stores to the Platting Committee for further study.

• Allison LeClair seconded the motion.

• Voice vote passes. Referred back to Platting Committee.

Zoning Reclassification of 1428 South Sandusky Street

Proponents – Jack Arnett, 1428 South Sandusky Street. Mr. Arnett is concerned because of

his property lines. Council President Kevin Myers and Law Director Brandon Gobrecht will

research and let Mr. Arnett know something at the Neighborhood Meeting scheduled for

April 16, 2026.

Proponents – Adam Frizzell

Opponents - None

• Robert Taylor made a motion to refer the Zoning reclassification of 1428 South

Sandusky from a designation Limited Industrial/Commercial (LIC) to Suburban

Residential (R1) to the Platting Committee for further study.

• Chris Mauritz seconded the motion.

• Voice vote passes. Referred back to Platting Committee.

Zoning Reclassification of 1518 South Sandusky Street

Proponents – None

Opponents – None

• Allison LeClair made a motion to refer the Zoning reclassification of 1518 South

Sandusky from a designation Limited Industrial/Commercial (LIC) to Suburban

Residential (R1) to the Platting Committee for further study.

• Cody Scott seconded the motion.

• Voice vote passes. Referred back to Platting Committee.

Zoning Reclassification of 1482 South Sandusky Street

Proponents – None

Opponents – None

• Chris Mauritz made a motion to refer the Zoning reclassification of 1482 South

Sandusky from a designation Limited Industrial/Commercial (LIC) to Suburban

Residential (R1) to the Platting Committee for further study.

• Allison LeClair seconded the motion.

• Voice vote passes. Referred back to Platting Committee.

James Mee made a motion to excuse Councilman Terry Spiegel who is absent from the

meeting. Seconded by Chris Mauritz. Voice vote passes.

Robert Taylor made a motion to adjourn the Public Hearing. Seconded by Allison LeClair.

Voice vote passes and meeting adjourned at 5:55 p.m.

Bucyrus City Regular Council Meeting

Tuesday, April 21, 2026

Audio can be found at: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=uKG6MQU2TGU

President Kevin Myers, Jim Mee, Robert Taylor, Ray Thitoff, Christopher Mauritz,

Allison LeClair, Terry Spiegel, Cody Scott

Call to Order

President Kevin Myers called the meeting to order at 6:00 pm.

The Pledge of Allegiance was recited.

Roll Call:

Roll Call taken with the following members present: James Mee, Robert Taylor, Ray Thitoff,

Christopher Mauritz, Allison LeClair, Terry Spiegel, Cody Scott

Accepting Minutes from Previous Sessions:

Minutes presented from the Regular Council Meeting on April 7, 2026

• Motion to approve: James Mee

• Second: Chris Mauritz

• Voice vote passes

Communication and Petitions:

The Tax Incentive Review Council (TIRC) sent a letter with their recommendations for tax

abatement/exemption agreement for PROPS 4 US LLC and BUCYRUS STORAGE COMPLEX.

• Terry Spiegel made a motion to refer the letters to Economic Development

• Seconded by James Mee

• Voice vote passes

Special Guests and Recognitions:

Police Chief Dorsey recognized dispatcher Kimberly Barker for her outstanding

performance and professionalism while handling a recent call involving an outside agency.

Citizen Participation:

Greg White, 1125 Hillcrest Drive. Mr. White shared his opinions on Proposed Ordinance 10-

2026 he feels that it still needs reviewed. He also suggests the Council have a Citizen

Advisory Committee and review the ethics policy.

James Cady, of Sandusky, Ohio. Mr. Cady inquiring about road paving in the city.

Standing Committee Reports

Platting –  – James Mee reports they had a Public Hearing on 4/14/26 and will discuss at the

next Platting Committee meeting.

Draft –  – Not Approved Page 1 of 7

Public Lands and Buildings – – No report

Health & Safety ––No report

Finance – – No report

Economic Development –  – No report

Service –  – Chris Mauritz reported that the attorney representing the city for the wastewater

project involving the Ohio EPA will be present at the Service Committee meeting on

4/23/26.

Traffic Commission –  – No report.

James Mee  made a motion to accept collectively the standing committee reports as

presented, with second from Cody Scott.  Motion passed by voice vote.

Report of City Officers

Mayor, Bruce Truka: Mayor Truka congratulated Ohio Mutual for merging with an Idaho

based company, thanked Peacock Water and Baker’s Pizza for their donations to the

Community Garden and reports the Land Bank has funds to help tear down houses. Mayor

Truka went over the grants the City of Bucyrus has applied for and used for 2026.

Service Safety Director, Tommy Starner: SSD Starner reported the City has implemented

a Public Records Policy and Bonnie Welch, Communications Director, will oversee the

Public Records Requests. SSD Starner would like referrals made to the Service Committee

for the Sugar Grove sewer rates, the Final Findings and Orders, and the agreement with

Ohio EPA. He would also like to refer to the Financial Capability Assessment to the

Finance Committee.

• James Mee made a motion to refer Sugar Grove sewer rates, the Final Findings and

Orders and the Ohio EPA agreement to Service Committee and the Financial

Capability Assessment to the Finance Committee.

• Seconded by Cody Scott

• Voice vote passes

Auditor, Kali Lewis: Auditor Lewis provided Council with a break down of potential revenue

from American Tower (Verizon) that she will discuss at the May 7, 2026 committee

meetings.

Treasurer, Adrienne Roski: Treasurer Roski submitted the March 2026 report.

• Terry Spiegel made a motion to accept the March 2026 report.

• Seconded by James Mee.

Police Chief Jon Dorsey: No report

Fire Chief Schwemley: No report

Law Director, Brandon Gobrecht: No report

presented, with a second from Chris Mauritz.  Motion passed by voice vote.

HEALTH AND SAFETY COMMITTEE

ORDINANCE NO. 22-2026

REPEALING CHAPTER 385, ENTITLED “REGULATION OF AMBULANCES””,

OF TITLE ELEVEN, ENTITLED LICENSING, OF PART THREE ENTITLED

“TRAFFIC CODE” OF THE BUCYRUS CODIFIED ORDINANCES,

REGULATION OF AMBULANCES.

WHEREAS, Chapter 385, entitled “regulation of ambulances”, of title

eleven entitled “licensing”, of part three entitled “traffic code” of the Bucyrus

Codified Ordinances governs the regulation of ambulances within the City; and

WHEREAS, Council has determined that Chapter 385 should be repealed

in its entirety;

NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT ORDAINED BY THE COUNCIL OF THE CITY OF

BUCYRUS, STATE OF OHIO, a majority of all members elected thereto

concurring:

SECTION 1: That Chapter 385 of the Bucyrus Codified Ordinances, Regulation

of Ambulances, is hereby repealed in its entirety.

SECTION 2: That this Ordinance shall take effect and be in force from and after

the earliest period allowed by law.

Passed this ____ day of ______, 2026.

ATTEST: _________________________

_________________________

Michelle Mostoller,  Kevin Myers,

Clerk of the Council President of Council

Approved this ____ day of ______, 2026.

_____________________

Bruce D. Truka – MAYOR

HEALTH AND SAFETY COMMITTEE

ORDINANCE NO. 23-2026

AMENDING AND SUPPLEMENTING CHAPTER 351 OF THE BUCYRUS

CODIFIED ORDINANCES BY REPEALING CERTAIN SECTIONS AND

RENUMBERING THE REMAINING SECTIONS ACCORDINGLY.

WHEREAS, Chapter 351 of the Bucyrus Codified Ordinances governs

parking regulations within the City; and

WHEREAS, Council has determined that certain sections of Chapter 351

should be repealed and that the remaining sections should be renumbered to

maintain consistency and avoid gaps within the Code;

NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT ORDAINED BY THE COUNCIL OF THE CITY OF

BUCYRUS, STATE OF OHIO, a majority of all members elected thereto

concurring:

SECTION 1. That Sections 351.10, 351.12, and 351.16 of the Bucyrus Codified

Ordinances are hereby repealed in their entirety.

SECTION 2. That the remaining sections of Chapter 351 shall be renumbered as

necessary to maintain a consistent and sequential order within the

Chapter.

SECTION 3. That this Ordinance shall take effect and be in force from and after

the earliest period allowed by law.

Passed this ____ day of ______, 2026.

ATTEST: _________________________

_________________________

Michelle Mostoller,  Kevin Myers,

Clerk of the Council President of Council

Approved this ____ day of ______, 2026.

_____________________

Bruce D. Truka – – MAYOR

HEALTH AND SAFETY COMMITTEE

ORDINANCE NO. 24-2026

AMENDING AND SUPPLEMENTING CHAPTER 135 OF THE BUCYRUS CODIFIED

ORDINANCES BY REPEALING CERTAIN SECTIONS AND RENUMBERING THE

REMAINING SECTIONS ACCORDINGLY.

WHEREAS, Chapter 135 of the Bucyrus Codified Ordinances governs provisions

within the City; and

WHEREAS, Council has determined that certain sections of Chapter 135 should

be repealed and that the remaining sections should be renumbered to maintain

consistency and avoid gaps within the Code;

NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT ORDAINED BY THE COUNCIL OF THE CITY OF

BUCYRUS, STATE OF OHIO, a majority of all members elected thereto concurring:

SECTION 1. That Sections 135.06 and 135.08 of the Bucyrus Codified Ordinances are

hereby repealed in their entirety.

SECTION 2. That the remaining sections of Chapter 135 shall be renumbered as

necessary to maintain a consistent and sequential order within the Chapter.

SECTION 3. That this Ordinance shall take effect and be in force from and after the

earliest period allowed by law.

agreement with the Crawford County Commissioners and the City of Galion for the

administration of the PY2026 Community Housing Impact Preservation (CHIP)

program (if funded) whereby the City of Bucyrus and the City of Galion will be the

Partners and Crawford County will be the Grantee of the Partnership; and

WHEREAS, this ordinance authorizes the Mayor to enter into the attached

agreement with the Crawford County Commissioners and the City of Galion and

authorizes the Commissioners to submit the 2026 CHIP Application on behalf of the

Partnership; and

WHEREAS, as the application is due by June 24, 2026, this Ordinance is

deemed to be an Emergency.

State of Ohio, two-thirds of all members thereto concurring:

BE IT RESOLVED BY THE COUNCIL OF THE CITY OF BUCYRUS, STATE OF

OHIO, two-thirds of all members elected thereto concurring:-

SECTION 1. The Mayor is authorized to enter into the attached partnership

agreement on behalf of the City of Bucyrus with Crawford County and the City

of Galion, and the Crawford County Commissioners are authorized to submit

the application for the PY2026 CHIP. The City of Bucyrus and the City of

Galion will each be the Partners, and Crawford County will be the Grantee.

The Grantee shall be solely responsible for the administration, regulatory

compliance, fiscal operations and all the terms of the grant agreement.

SECTION 2. That this Ordinance is hereby declared to be an emergency measure to

allow the entire process of applying for and setting up the Program Year 2026

Community Housing Impact Preservation program for the June 24, 2026

deadline, same being deemed necessary for the preservation of the health,

HEALTH AND SAFETY COMMITTEE

ORDINANCE NO. 28-2026

AN ORDINANCE IMPLEMENTING A MORATORIUM ON THE DEVELOPMENT OF

DATA CENTERS WITHIN THE CITY OF BUCYRUS, OHIO, AND DECLARING AN

EMERGENCY.

WHEREAS, the need for centers containing networked computer servers

typically used by organizations for the remote storage, processing, or distribution of

large amounts of data, colloquially known as “Data Centers”, are growing throughout

the United States and Northeast Ohio; and

WHEREAS, while the need for them grows, Data Centers carry with them a

number of risks to the community; and

WHEREAS, Data Centers require large amounts of electricity and/or other

sources of power which can place a burden on the local supply and drive up costs of

electricity and/or other implemented power sources for others in the community,

potentially resulting in brown outs or blackouts; and

WHEREAS, Data Center utilize large amounts of water which can place a

burden on the local supply and drive of costs or result in shortages for others in the

community; and

WHEREAS, just like Data Centers utilize large amounts of water, their

sewage needs are substantial. Their sewer needs can place an undue burden on

others in the community and pose a risk to sewage infrastructure due to the

corrosive nature of the Data Center’s waste water;

WHEREAS, Data Centers produce ambient noise at substantial levels which

will need to be regulated and mitigated if one were to be erected.

NOW THEREFORE,

BE IT ORDAINED BY THE COUNCIL OF THE CITY OF BUCYRUS, STATE OF

OHIO, two-thirds of all members elected thereto concurring:-

SECTION 1. That a renewable, two-year moratorium shall commence as soon as

legally permissible prohibiting the construction or development of a Data Center

within the City. This City shall not issue any zoning permits, building permits, or

other permits to any person or entity seeking to open a data center during the life of

this moratorium. For purposes of this moratorium, the definition of “Data Center”

shall mean “a facility used or to be used primarily to house computer data center

equipment used or to be used in conducting one or more computer data center

businesses”. “Data Center Equipment” shall mean tangible personal property used

or to be used for any of the following” (a) to conduct a computer data center

business, including equipment cooling systems to manage the performance of

computer data center equipment; (b) to generate, transform, transmit, distribute, or

manage electricity necessary to operate the tangible personal property used or to be

used in conducting a computer data center business; or, (c) as building and

construction materials sold to construction contractors for incorporation into a

computer data center.

SECTION 2. The Administration is directed to consider the impact of Data Centers

within the City and make recommendations to Council on how best to

appropriately regulate and zone Data Centers within the City. Key areas of

concern include, but at not limited to, the following: electrical needs and

usage, their impact on the local power grid, their ability to generate energy

without impairing other resources (i.e. gas), their impact on local

telecommunications services, their impact on City water resources, their

ability to recycle water, their impact on City waste water services, their ability

to treat water, their impact regarding noise pollution, their viability within

brownfields, their anticipated annual revenue generation, etc.

SECTION 3. The Administration may continue to utilize outside legal counsel

services based upon a potential conflict of interest identified with the Law

Department. Payments shall first be made of the City’s discretionary fund

and/or professional services fund. In the event such legal expense exceed

$15,000.00, the Administration is directed to request further guidance from

Council.

SECTION 4. That, in the event any portion of this Ordinance is found unlawful, said

portion shall be severed from this Ordinance and interpreted in a manner to

give it the fullest effect possible in a manner consistent with the intent of this

Ordinance.

SECTION 5. That existing Data Centers shall be grandfathered in under this

Ordinance.

SECTION 6. It is found and determined that all formal actions of this Council

concerning and relating to the adoption of this Ordinance were adopted in an

open meeting of this Council, and that all deliberations of this Council and any

of its committees that resulted in such formal action were in meetings open to

the public in compliance with all legal requirements, including Section 121.22

of the Ohio Revised Code.

SECTION 7. That this Ordinance is necessary for the immediate preservation of the

public peace, health, or safety in within the City and is hereby declared to be

an emergency to protect our citizens, utilities and their rates, and local

infrastructure. Therefore, if this Ordinance shall become effective immediately

upon passage after receiving an affirmative vote of two-thirds vote of all the

members elected to the legislative authority. If passed by a lesser majority,

this Ordinance shall become effective at the earliest point allowed by law.

Passed this 4th day of May, 2026.

ATTEST: _________________________

_________________________

Michelle Mostoller,

Clerk of the Council

Approved this 4th day of May, 2026

_____________________

Bruce D. Truka – MAYOR

Kevin Myers,

President of Council
